A.2
Proof of
\cref
lem:d-comm

We will need the following

Lemma 39
.

Let
f\colon X\to DY
. Then

1.

\lat f^{\star}=(\lat f)^{\star}=f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat
;

2.

\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\lat)=\lat\hskip 3.44444pt\tau
.

Proof.

For the first clause, note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}[f,(\lat f)^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}=

\displaystyle\;[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444ptf,[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat f,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}(\lat f)^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[f,(\lat f)^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}},

which implies
[f,(\lat f)^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}=f^{\star}
, for the resulting equation
is uniquely satisfied by
f^{\star}
. Now,
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}(\lat f)^{\star}=[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\lat f,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}(\lat f)^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[f,(\lat f)^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}=\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}(\lat f^{\star})
,
which implies
\lat f^{\star}=(\lat f)^{\star}
, since
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}
is an isomorphism.

Analogously,
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat=[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat=[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}=\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat f^{\star}
,
and therefore
f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat=\lat f^{\star}
.

Let us proceed with the second clause. By definition,

\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau=(\mathsf{id}+\tau)\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})
, which implies

\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt)=\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\tau)\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\lat)=

\displaystyle\;\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt)\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\tau)\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\tau)\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\lat\tau,

and we are done.
∎

Let us continue with the proof of
\cref
lem:d-comm.
It is easy to see that the dual
\hat{\tau}\colon DX\times Y\to D(X\times Y)
of
\tau
is
a final coalgebra morphism from
(DX\times Y,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\mathsf{id}):DX\times Y\to X\times Y+DX\times Y)
to

(D(X\times Y),\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})
where
\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}
is the obvious dual of
\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}
. We need to
to check that
\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau=\tau^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}
. Using the elementary
properties of strength, distributivity transformations and
\cref
lem:later,
we obtain:

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau

\displaystyle\;=[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hat{\tau}^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau

\displaystyle\;=[\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hat{\tau}^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\tau)\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\mathsf{id}),\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\times\mathsf{id})]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hat{\tau}^{\star}D(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\times\mathsf{id})\tau]\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\hat{\tau}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\times\mathsf{id}))^{\star}\tau]\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})^{\star}\tau]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau}))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(D\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau}))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt[(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\tau,(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\tau]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt[(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})^{\star}\tau,(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\eta^{\star}\tau,(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,(\lat\hat{\tau})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}).

That is,
\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau
is a fixpoint of

\displaystyle f\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt[\tau,\,\lat f]]\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}).

(4)

on
\mathbf{C}(DX\times DY,D(X\times Y))
.
By a symmetric argument, also
\tau^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}
is a fixpoint of the same map.
Let us denote
(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})
by
w
. For every fixpoint
f

of (
4
):

\displaystyle f=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat f]]\hskip 3.44444ptw

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt\hskip 3.44444pt[[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444ptf],\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\eta,f]^{\star}]\hskip 3.44444pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}+(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\tau,\lat\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}]]\hskip 3.44444ptw

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle[\eta,f]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}+(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\tau,\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}]]\hskip 3.44444ptw

and, clearly,

\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\kern-0.5pt1}\kern-1.0pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}+(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[(D\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau,\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}]]\hskip 3.44444ptw\colon DX\times DY\to D(X\times Y+DX\times DY)

is guarded. By
\cref
prop:D-props (4) this implies
\tau^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}=\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau
.
∎

A.3
Proof of
\cref
prop:fold

Of course, the first two axioms are just corresponding instances of their
guarded versions. We only need to show that
Folding
is equivalent to

Compositionality
in presence of the other two axioms. Let us first equivalently
reformulate the
Folding
axiom. Note that by uniformity,

[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}=f^{\sharp}
and also by
Fixpoint
,

(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{}\linebreak(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt(f^{\sharp}+h)\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}=f^{\sharp}
.
Analogously,
(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{}\linebreak(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt(f^{\sharp}+h)\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth
,
and subsequently, by uniformity,
(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=((f^{\sharp}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pth)^{\sharp}
.
We have thus equivalently reduced
Folding
to

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=((f^{\sharp}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pth)^{\sharp}.

Now, the task of checking that this is equivalent to
Compositionality
amounts
to showing that

\displaystyle([(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}]\hskip 3.44444pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},h])^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}

follows from
Fixpoint
and
Uniformity
. Indeed, by
Uniformity
,

\displaystyle([(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}]\hskip 3.44444pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},h])^{\sharp}=\;

\displaystyle((\mathsf{id}+[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},h])[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}])^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},h]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle([(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth])^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},h],

hence

\displaystyle([(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}]\hskip 3.44444pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},h])^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=\;

\displaystyle([(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth])^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth.

On the other hand, using
Fixpoint
,

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=\;

\displaystyle[\mathsf{id},[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}]\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth.

In summary, we obtain the desired identity.
∎

A.4
Proof of
\cref
lem:b-mon

Existence of all free unguarded uniform-iteration algebras yields an adjunction between

\mathbf{C}
and the category of unguarded uniform-iteration algebras over
\mathbf{C}
. Let us call
the latter category
\mathbf{E}
and the corresponding adjunction
U\vdash F
. The only
nonobvious condition of Beck’s monadicity theorem is existence and preservation of
U
-split
coequalizers. Consider a split coequalizer

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces UA}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces UB}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Ug}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Uf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}

(5)

in
\mathbf{C}
, i.e. for suitable
t\colon UB\to UA
,
s\colon Z\to UB
,
e\hskip 3.44444pts=\mathsf{id}
,
s\hskip 3.44444ptt=g\hskip 3.44444ptt
,
and
f\hskip 3.44444ptt=\mathsf{id}
. Let
C
be uniform-iteration algebra whose carrier is
Z
and
whose iteration operator is defined as follows
(h\colon X\to Z+X)^{\hat{\sharp}}=e\hskip 3.44444pt((s+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pth)^{\sharp}
.
Hence
e
lifts to an uniform-iteration algebra morphism, which is moreover a coequalizer
of
f
and
g
in
\mathbf{E}
. The image of the resulting coequalizer in
\mathbf{E}
is
precisely (
5
), and thus we have shown that
U
preserves
U
-split
coequalizers. Therefore,
L
extends to a monad whose category of algebras
is precisely
\mathbf{E}
.
∎

A.5
Proof of
\cref
lem:stable

Assume stability of
KY
.
It is easy to check
that
\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\colon X\times A\to X
is a uniform-iteration algebra in
\mathbf{C}/X
, which implies
that
\mathsf{id}\times\eta\colon X\times Y\to X\times KY
is the unit morphism for
X\times KY
.
Let us fix some
f\colon X\times Y\to A
and note that
\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},f\rangle\colon X\times Y\to X\times A

is a morphism in
\mathbf{C}/X
. Using the universal property of
\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\colon X\times A\to X
in
\mathbf{C}/X
we obtain a unique iteration preserving
morphism
u\colon X\times KY\to X\times A
in
\mathbf{C}/X
for which the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times KY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\times\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},f\rangle}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}}

commutes. This entails that
u
is of the form
\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},f^{\hash}\rangle
where the
requisite property of
f^{\hash}
follows from the fact that
u
is iteration
preserving in
\mathbf{C}/X
. Conversely, from a unique
f^{\hash}
, subject to the declared
properties we can render
u
as
\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},f^{\hash}\rangle
.
∎

A.6
Proof of
\cref
lem:key

It suffices to show that

\displaystyle(h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times(\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)\hskip 3.44444pt\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},\mathsf{id}\rangle=(\mathsf{id}+\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},\mathsf{id}\rangle)\hskip 3.44444pt(h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf

from which the claim follows by uniformity. After simplifications we obtain

\displaystyle(h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},(\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf\rangle=(h+\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},\mathsf{id}\rangle)\hskip 3.44444ptf,

which is verified directly as follows:

\displaystyle\quad(h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}

\displaystyle\hskip 3.44444pt(((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(z),(\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt(f(z)))

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(z)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}(p,q)\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\hskip 3.44444pth(((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(z),q)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}

\displaystyle\qquad\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}(((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(z),z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(z)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}(p,q)\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\hskip 3.44444pth([\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}](f(z)),q)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}

/​​/
Fixpoint

\displaystyle\qquad\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}([\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}](f(z)),z^{\prime})

/​​/
Fixpoint

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(z)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}(p,q)\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\hskip 3.44444pth([\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}]\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}(p,q),q)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}

\displaystyle\qquad\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}([\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}]\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(z)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}(p,q)\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}(p,q)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}

\displaystyle\qquad\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}(((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(z^{\prime}),z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(h+\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},\mathsf{id}\rangle)(f(z)).

This completes the proof.
∎

A.7
Proof of
\cref
thm:K-lift

Lemma 40
.

{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}
is a commutative monad.

Proof.

Let
\tau\colon X\times KY\to K(X\times Y)
be strength of
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}
and let

\hat{\tau}\colon KX\times Y\to K(X\times Y)
be its obvious transpose. We need to
show that
\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau=\tau^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}
. Note that

KX\times KY
is an uniform-iteration algebra by
\cref
lem:prod_exp (1). It is easy to
see that both diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KX\times KY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(X\times Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\star}\hskip 2.41112pt\hat{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\times\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KX\times KY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(X\times Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 2.41112pt\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\times\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

commute. It is therefore sufficient for obtaining the desired identity

\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau=\tau^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}
to show that both
\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau

and
\tau^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}
are iteration preserving. We confine to the former case,
from which the second case is obtained by a symmetric argument.
Let
f\colon Z\to KX\times KY+Z
. We need to show that

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},((\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}\rangle=((\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}.

This in fact essentially follows from
\cref
lem:key:

\displaystyle((\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}=\;

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt((\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt((\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times(\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},\mathsf{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times((\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp})\hskip 3.44444pt\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},\mathsf{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp},((\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}\rangle.

This completes the proof.
∎

Let us continue the proof of the theorem.

We have already shown that
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}
is strong and commutative. It remains to
establish the law (
1
).
Since
K\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle=(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle)^{\star}
, using the definition
of Kleisli star for
K
, it suffices to show that
\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta
is a unique
iteration preserving morphism for which the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(KX\times X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau\hskip 2.41112pt\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\hskip 2.41112pt\langle\eta,\,\mathsf{id}\rangle}

commutes. Indeed,
\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta\hskip 3.44444pt\eta=\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\eta)\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle=\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle
, and

\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta
is iteration preserving by
\cref
lem:tau-Delta.
∎

A.8
Restriction Categories and Equational Lifting Monads

Recall the axioms of restriction categories
[
14
]
for further
reference.

\displaystyle f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)

\displaystyle\;=f

(
RST
1
)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g)

\displaystyle\;=(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)

(
RST
2
)

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(g^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f))

\displaystyle\;=(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)

(
RST
3
)

\displaystyle(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}h)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle\;=f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(h^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf)

(
RST
4
)

where
f\colon X\to KY
,
g\colon X\to KZ
and
h\colon Y\to KZ
.

For the rest of the section, let us fix an equational lifting monad
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}
, whose
Kleisli category
\mathbf{C}_{{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}}
is thus a restriction category. We then collect miscellaneous
facts about
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}
for further reference.

Lemma 41
.

Given
f\colon X\to TZ
,
{g\colon Y\to TZ}
,

\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}[f,g]=[(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f,(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g]
.

Proof.

By definition,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}[f,g]=

\displaystyle\;(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},[f,g]\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt[\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},f\rangle,\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}},g\rangle]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},f\rangle,(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}},g\rangle]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444pt(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},f\rangle,(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f,(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g]

and we are done.
∎

Lemma 42
.

For any
f\colon X\to TY
,
(T\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf=Tf\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)
.

Proof.

Note that
Tf\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)=(\eta\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta\hskip 3.44444ptf)
, and hence,
by
RST4
,
Tf\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)=(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}\mathsf{id})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta\hskip 3.44444ptf=(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf
.
Since
\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}\mathsf{id}=(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\mathsf{id}\rangle=(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444ptT\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle=T\eta
,
and we are done.
∎

Lemma 43
.

\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},T\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle=\mathsf{id}
.

Proof.

Indeed,

\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},T\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle=\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptT(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta=\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptT(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\hskip 3.44444ptT\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle=\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptT\langle\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle=(\hat{\tau}\hskip 3.44444pt(\eta\times\mathsf{id}))^{\star}=\eta^{\star}=\mathsf{id}.

∎

Lemma 44
.

Suppose that
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}
is equipped with
an operator
(\operatorname{-\!-})^{\dagger}\colon\mathbf{C}(X,T(Y+X))\to\mathbf{C}(X,TY)
that satisfies

Fixpoint
, and
Uniformity
. Then for any
f\colon X\to T(Y+X)
,
f^{\dagger}=(f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Using
Uniformity
,
f^{\dagger}=(T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\dagger}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta
.
Next, using
\cref
lem:dom-eta,

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}=

\displaystyle\;(f^{\dagger})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star})^{\dagger}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star})^{\dagger})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(T\eta)\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star})^{\dagger})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptT(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star})^{\dagger}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}).

We then show that

\displaystyle(T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star})^{\dagger}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})=(f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\dagger}.

(6)

This will entail the goal using
Fixpoint
as follows:

\displaystyle f^{\dagger}=

\displaystyle\;((T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star})^{\dagger}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\dagger})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\eta,(f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\dagger}]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\eta,(f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\dagger}]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})^{\star}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}))^{\dagger}.

We show (
6
) by
Uniformity
after establishing another auxiliary property:

\displaystyle f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})=[\eta\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

Indeed, by
Fixpoint
and
RST4
,

\displaystyle f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})=

\displaystyle\;f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}([\eta,f^{\dagger}]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}[\eta,f^{\dagger}])^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},[\eta,f^{\dagger}]\rangle)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt[\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\eta\rangle,\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}},f^{\dagger}\rangle]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\mathsf{id}\rangle,(T\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}},f^{\dagger}\rangle]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf.

Finally,

\displaystyle T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}

\displaystyle\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;T(\mathsf{id}+\eta)\hskip 3.44444pt[\eta\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\eta\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt(T\eta)\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\eta\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},(T\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444ptT(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})]^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf

/​​/
\cref
lem:dom-eta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;T(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger})\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f^{\dagger}),

which entails (
6
) by
Uniformity
.
∎

A.9
Proof of
\cref
prop:K-enriched

Lemma 45
.

For any
f\colon Z\to Y+Z
,

\displaystyle\bigl(X\times Z\,\xrightarrow{(\eta\hskip 2.41112pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 2.41112pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 2.41112pt(\mathsf{id}\times f)}\,KX+X\times Z\bigr)^{\sharp}~~\sqsubseteq~~\bigl(X\times Z\,\xrightarrow{\eta\hskip 2.41112pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}}\,KX\bigr).

Proof.

The goal is equivalent to

\displaystyle((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}=(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}).

By transforming the right-hand expression as follows

\displaystyle(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})

\displaystyle\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}\bigl(((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\bigr)

\displaystyle\;=(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\rangle

\displaystyle\;=K(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\bigl((\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))\bigr)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle\;=\bigl((K(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))\bigr)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle\;=\bigl((K(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\eta(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))\bigr)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle\;=\bigl((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))\bigr)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

Next,

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}

\displaystyle\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\hskip 3.44444pt(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))

\displaystyle\;=(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})+\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))

\displaystyle\;=(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times(\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))

\displaystyle\;=(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times f\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})

\displaystyle\;=(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f)\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}),

Hence, by uniformity,

\displaystyle((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+

\displaystyle\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\;=((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle\;=((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f)))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle\;=(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp},

and we are done.
∎

We are left to show that the order and the bottom elements are respected by the
Kleisli composition.

Right monotonicity of composition:
Let
f\sqsubseteq g
, i.e.
f=g\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)
. Then

h\hskip 3.44444ptg\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(h\hskip 3.44444ptf)=h\hskip 3.44444ptg\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(h\hskip 3.44444ptg\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f))=h\hskip 3.44444ptg\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(h\hskip 3.44444ptg)\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)=h\hskip 3.44444ptg\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)=h\hskip 3.44444ptf
,
hence
h\hskip 3.44444ptf\sqsubseteq h\hskip 3.44444ptg
.

Left monotonicity of composition:
Analogously, if
f=g\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)
then

g\hskip 3.44444ptu\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(f\hskip 3.44444ptu)=g\hskip 3.44444ptu\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(g\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)\hskip 3.44444ptu)=g\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(g\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f))\hskip 3.44444ptu=g\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g)\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)\hskip 3.44444ptu=g\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)\hskip 3.44444ptu=f\hskip 3.44444ptu
, hence

f\hskip 3.44444ptu\sqsubseteq g\hskip 3.44444ptu
.

Monotonicity of strength:
Suppose that
f\sqsubseteq g
, i.e.
f=g^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)
. Then

\displaystyle\qquad(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle

\displaystyle)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},f\rangle)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},f\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\langle\mathsf{id},\mathsf{id}\rangle,f\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444ptK(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\langle\mathsf{id},\mathsf{id}\rangle,f\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},f\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times g)\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\langle\mathsf{id},\mathsf{id}\rangle,f\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times g\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},f\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times g))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},f\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times g))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times g^{\star})\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f\rangle

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}f)\rangle.

Strictness of strength:

\displaystyle\qquad\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\bot)=\;

\displaystyle\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}^{\sharp})

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle((\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}))^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle((\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}^{\sharp}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\bot.

Right strictness of composition:
The equation
f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\bot=\bot
follows from the fact that
f^{\star}
preserves
iteration.

Left strictness of composition:
The equation
\bot^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf=\bot
is much more subtle. First, observe
that
\bot\hskip 3.44444ptf=\bot
, which easily follows by uniformity. We are left to show that

{\bot^{\star}=\bot}
. Since
\bot\hskip 3.44444pt\eta=\bot^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta=\bot
, by definition of the
lifting
\bot^{\star}
, it suffices to show that
\bot
is iteration preserving,
i.e. for any
f\colon X\to KY+X
,
\bot\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\sharp}=((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}
,
equivalently, that
{((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}=\bot}
. Note that

((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}=((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta
.
Indeed,

\displaystyle(\bot+\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f)=(\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}},

hence, by uniformity,

\displaystyle((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}=((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\Delta=((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta.

Now,

\displaystyle((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}=\;

\displaystyle((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle((\bot^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\bot^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt((\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times f))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\;

\displaystyle\bot^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta

/​​/
\cref
lem:strict, monotonicity

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\bot.

Therefore, indeed,
((\bot+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}=\bot
.
∎

A.10
Proof of
\cref
prop:copy

For the identity, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta=

\displaystyle\;\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptK\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(\hat{\tau}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\eta,\mathsf{id}\rangle)^{\star}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta)^{\star}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;K\Delta.

For the inequality, note that

\displaystyle(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}^{\star}

\displaystyle\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle f,g\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle f,g\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\hskip 3.44444pt(f\times\mathsf{id}))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},g\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g).

Hence,
(K\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle f,g\rangle=f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}g)\sqsubseteq f^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta=f
.
∎

A.11
Proof of
\cref
thm:klee

For the first clause we need to show that
f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}=f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}
. Since

\displaystyle(f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}})\hskip 3.44444pt(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}})=

\displaystyle\;f^{\sharp}x\downharpoonright\bot

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\bot,

\displaystyle(f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}})\hskip 3.44444pt(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}})\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}y\mapsto f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(x)\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}y\mapsto f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(y)\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}(y,n),

f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}
satisfies the definition for
f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}
, and
therefore we obtain the identity in question.

We proceed with the second clause. Suppose that
f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}\sqsubseteq g\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}
,
and show
f^{\sharp}\sqsubseteq g
.
The idea is to introduce such
h\colon X\times{\rm Nature}\to K{\rm Nature}+X\times{\rm Nature}
that

h^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}})
runs like
f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(x)
, but instead of the final result of

f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(x)
delivers the number of steps needed to reach the result. We then
show that
f^{\sharp}=(f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},h^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle
,
which entails the desired property:

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle\;(f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},h^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\;(g\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},h^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle g,h^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;g\downharpoonright h^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\;g.

Thus we are left to produce the requisite
h
. Let

h=(\eta\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}\hskip 3.44444pt(f\times\operatorname{\mathsf{s}})
. We then have

\displaystyle(f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}

\displaystyle\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},h^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times h^{\sharp})\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt((\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times h))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle

/​​/
\cref
prop:tau-prop

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;(((f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times h))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id},\langle\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}\operatorname{!}\rangle\rangle

We are left to show that the latter is equal to
f^{\sharp}
. We strengthen the
goal slightly and show

\displaystyle(((f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times h))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},\langle w,\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle\rangle=f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444ptw

(7)

instead, where
w\colon X\times{\rm Nature}\to X
is defined by primitive recursion as follows:

\displaystyle w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}})=x,

\displaystyle w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)=\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto x\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto w(y,n).

We will need the following facts:

\displaystyle w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(x,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto w(x,n)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto y,

(8)

\displaystyle f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(x,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto y,

(9)

which both follow by induction. The former one follows from

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(

\displaystyle x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}})\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto y=w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(

\displaystyle x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto

\displaystyle\qquad\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(y,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z\mapsto z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto x\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto

\displaystyle\qquad\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(y,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto w(y,n)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z\mapsto z.

To show (
9
), observe that

\displaystyle f(w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n))=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto f(w(y,n))

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto f(w(y,n)).

Hence,

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(

\displaystyle x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto\bot=f(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}\operatorname{\mathsf{o}}),

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(

\displaystyle x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto y

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto

\displaystyle\qquad\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(y,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto\bot.

Now,

\displaystyle(((f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}

\displaystyle\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times h))\hskip 3.44444pt(x,(w(x,n),n))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(x,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}(x,(y,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(x,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}(x,(w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n),\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n))

/​​/ (
8
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(w(x,n))\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}z\mapsto\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}(x,(w(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n),\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)).

/​​/ (

)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{s}},f\hskip 3.44444ptw\rangle)\hskip 3.44444pt(x,n).

Uniformity
,

\displaystyle(((f^{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times h))^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},\langle w,\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle\rangle=((\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{s}},f\hskip 3.44444ptw\rangle)^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\qquad((\mathsf{id}+w)\hskip 3.44444pt((

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{s}},f\hskip 3.44444ptw\rangle))\hskip 3.44444pt(x,n)

/​​/ (
8
)

\displaystyle\;f(w(x,n)),

from which, again by

, we obtain

\displaystyle((\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\mathsf{id}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{s}},f\hskip 3.44444ptw\rangle)^{\sharp}=f^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444ptw.

Corollary 46
.

Each
KX
is a free Elgot algebra on
X
.

Proof.

By
\cref
prop:fold, it remains to show
Folding
. Let

h\colon Y\to X+Y
and
f\colon X\to KZ+X
. It easily follows by uniformity
that
[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}=f^{\sharp}
. Then

\displaystyle[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}=\;

\displaystyle[\mathsf{id},[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle[f^{\sharp},[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle[\mathsf{id},[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt(f^{\sharp}+h),

\displaystyle(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}=\;

\displaystyle[\mathsf{id},(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt(f^{\sharp}+h)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle[[\mathsf{id},f^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle[[\mathsf{id},(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}]\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth]

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle[\mathsf{id},(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth].

That is, both
[(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444ptf,\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pth]^{\sharp}
and

(f^{\sharp}+h)^{\sharp}
mutually satisfy the fixpoint identities of each other.
Hence, by
\cref
thm:klee they are mutually smaller under
\sqsubseteq
, and hence
equal.
∎

A.12
Proof of
\cref
cor:klee

Suppose that
[\mathsf{id},g]\hskip 3.44444ptf\sqsubseteq g
for some
g\colon X\to KY
, i.e.
[\mathsf{id},g]\hskip 3.44444ptf=g^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dom}}([\mathsf{id},g]\hskip 3.44444ptf)
.
This yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto g(y)=\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto g(x)\downharpoonright r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto g(x)\downharpoonright g(y)

This entails

\displaystyle g(x)\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{o}})=

\displaystyle\;\bot,

\displaystyle g(x)\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}(x,\operatorname{\mathsf{s}}n)=

\displaystyle\;\operatorname{\mathsf{case}}\mathbin{}f(x)\operatorname{\mathsf{of}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}r\mapsto r\operatorname{\mathsf{\kern-1.0pt;}}\mathbin{}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}y\mapsto g(x)\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}(y,n).

By induction,
f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}=g\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\downharpoonright f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}
, i.e.
f^{{\langle\kern-0.8pt\sharp}}\sqsubseteq g\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}
,
hence, by
\cref
thm:klee,
f^{\sharp}\sqsubseteq g
.
∎

A.13
Proof of
\cref
thm:K-pre-Elgot

We build on
\cref
cor:K-pre-Elgot. To show that
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}
is pre-Elgot,
we are left to check the remaining property:
h^{\star}\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\sharp}=((h^{\star}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}
where
f\colon Z\to KX+Z
and
h\colon X\to KY
.
This is, in fact a consequence of
Compositionality
:

\displaystyle((h^{\star}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\,=(((\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\hskip 3.44444pth^{\star})^{\sharp}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}

\displaystyle\,=([(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\hskip 3.44444pth^{\star},\operatorname{}\linebreak\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}]\hskip 3.44444pt{[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\operatorname{}\linebreak f]})^{\sharp}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}

\displaystyle\,=((h^{\star}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\hskip 3.44444pt{[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\operatorname{}\linebreak f]})^{\sharp}\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}

\displaystyle\,=((h^{\star}+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp}.

Now, given any pre-Elgot monad
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}
, for every
X
we define
\alpha_{X}\colon KX\to TX

as the unique Elgot algebra morphism such that
\alpha_{X}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta_{X}=\eta_{X}
by

\cref
cor:K-pre-Elgot. Naturality of
\alpha_{X}
in
X
follows from
the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces KY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces TX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Kf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Tf}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

where we make use of the fact that both
Kf=(\eta\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\star}
and

Tf=(\eta\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\star}
are iteration preserving since both
K
and
T

are pre-Elgot, hence both
\alpha\hskip 3.44444pt(Kf)
and
(Tf)\hskip 3.44444pt\alpha
are
iteration preserving. Analogously, since for every
f\colon X\to KY
,
f^{\star}

is iteration preserving and
\alpha\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\eta=\alpha\hskip 3.44444ptf=(\alpha\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\alpha\hskip 3.44444pt\eta
,

\alpha\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\star}=(\alpha\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\alpha
,
\alpha
respects
Kleisli lifting.

Finally, to show that
{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}
is initial strong pre-Elgot, we are left to show that
for strong pre-Elgot
{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}
, the induced
\alpha\colon{\bm{\mathsf{K}}}\to{\bm{\mathsf{T}}}
respects strength,
i.e.
\alpha\hskip 3.44444pt\tau=\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\alpha)
. Since
\alpha\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\eta)=\alpha\hskip 3.44444pt\eta=\eta=\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\eta)=\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\alpha)\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\eta)
,
we are done by stability of the
KX
.
∎

A.14
Proof of
\cref
prop:uniform-iteration-eq

Let us show the first clause. First, let us check that given
a\colon DA\to A
, the induced operator
(\operatorname{-\!-})^{\sharp}
indeed satisfies the axioms of uniform-iteration algebras.

Fixpoint
Let every
f\colon X\to A+X
. Then

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle\;a\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;a\hskip 3.44444pt[\operatorname{\mathsf{now}},\lat]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[a\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{now}},a\hskip 3.44444pt\lat]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\mathsf{id},a]\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\mathsf{id},a\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)]\hskip 3.44444ptf

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;[\mathsf{id},f^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444ptf,

Uniformity
Let
f\colon X\to A+X
,
g\colon Y\to A+Y
and
h\colon X\to Y
, and
assume that
(\mathsf{id}+h)\hskip 3.44444ptf=g\hskip 3.44444pth
. We have to show that
f^{\sharp}=g^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth
.
Indeed,

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle\;a\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;a\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}g)\hskip 3.44444pth

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;g^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pth.

Conversely, given a uniform-iteration algebra structure
(A,(\operatorname{-\!-})^{\sharp})
,
observe that
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}=\mathsf{id}
and
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}
.

Let us show that the given passages are mutually inverse. On the one hand,

{a\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})=a}
, and on the other hand,
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)=f^{\sharp}

by uniformity, since
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)=(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)\hskip 3.44444ptf
.

Finally, given two uniform-iteration algebras
(A,(\operatorname{-\!-})^{\sharp})
and

(B,(\operatorname{-\!-})^{\sharp^{\prime}})
and a morphism
h\colon A\to B
, show that iteration
preservation by
h
is equivalent to being a
D
-algebra morphism. If
h
is
iteration preserving then
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt(Dh)=(h+Dh)\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}
, which by uniformity
entails
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(Dh)=((h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})^{\sharp}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle h\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}=((h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})^{\sharp^{\prime}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(Dh),

i.e.
h
is a
D
-coalgebra morphism. Conversely, if
h
is a
D
-algebra morphism,
then for any
f\colon X\to A+X
,

\displaystyle h\hskip 3.44444ptf^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle\;h\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}})^{\sharp^{\prime}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;((h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf)^{\sharp^{\prime}},

where the last step is by uniformity, for

(h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)=(h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)\hskip 3.44444ptf=(\mathsf{id}+\operatorname{\mathsf{coit}}f)\hskip 3.44444pt(h+\mathsf{id})\hskip 3.44444ptf
.

Let us proceed with the second clause. In the guarded case, the requisite equivalence between Elgot algebras and

{\bm{\mathsf{D}}}
-algebras is shown previously
[
18
, Theorem 5.7]
. We are left
to check that the “unguardedness condition”
a=[\mathsf{id},a]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}
of
search-algebras
(A,a\colon DA\to A)
corresponds to the requirement that
\hat{a}=\mathsf{id}
on
the respective
\operatorname{Id}
-guarded Elgot algebras
(A,\hat{a}\colon A\to A,(\operatorname{-\!-})^{\sharp})
.
The involved connection between
a
and
\hat{a}
is precisely:
a=\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}
.
Now, if
\hat{a}=\mathsf{id}
then
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}
and if

\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}=[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}
then
\hat{a}=[\mathsf{id},[\hat{a},\hat{a}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{a}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}=[\mathsf{id},\hat{a}\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}=\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}=[\mathsf{id},[\mathsf{id},\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}^{\sharp}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}=\mathsf{id}
.
∎

A.15
{\accentset{\scalebox{.9}[1]{\texttildelow}}{D}}
is a strong functor

{\accentset{\scalebox{.9}[1]{\texttildelow}}{D}}
is a strong functor and
\rho
is a strong natural transformation.

Proof.

Recall that
D
is strong with a strength
\tau\colon X\times DY\to D(X\times Y)
.
By the axioms of strength,
\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\iota^{\star})=(\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\iota))^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau
, and it is easy to obtain by coinduction
that
\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\iota)=\iota\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{assoc}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\mathsf{\kern-0.5pt1}}
. In summary,

\tau\hskip 3.44444pt(\mathsf{id}\times\iota^{\star})=(\iota\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{assoc}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\mathsf{\kern-0.5pt1}})^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau=\iota^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt(D\operatorname{\mathsf{assoc}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\mathsf{\kern-0.5pt1}})\hskip 3.44444pt\tau
.
Using the fact that (
2
) is preserved by products, we introduce strength for
{\accentset{\scalebox{.9}[1]{\texttildelow}}{D}}

as the universal map in

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times D(Y\times{\rm Nature})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times DY}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times{\accentset{\scalebox{.9}[1]{\texttildelow}}{D}}Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D((X\times Y)\times{\rm Nature})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(X\times Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\accentset{\scalebox{.9}[1]{\texttildelow}}{D}}(X\times Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\times D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\times\iota^{\star}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(D\operatorname{\mathsf{assoc}}^{\text{\kern 0.5pt-}\mathsf{\kern-0.5pt1}})\hskip 2.41112pt\tau_{X,Y\times{\rm Nature}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{id}\times\rho_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{X,Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\star}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{X\times Y}}

The axioms of strength then follow automatically, as well as the fact that

\rho
is a strong natural transformation.
∎

Lemma 47
.

The morphism
\langle D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle\colon D(X\times Y)\to DX\times DY
is a section.

Proof.

We define the requisite retraction
DX\times DY\to D(X\times Y)
as the composition

w^{\sharp}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\colon DX\times DY\to D(X\times Y)
where

\displaystyle w=\bigl(D(X\times Y)\,\xrightarrow{[\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\,\lat\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}]\hskip 2.41112pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}}\,D(X\times Y+D(X\times Y))\bigr).

It is easy to check that
\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}w
factors through
(\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}+\mathsf{id})
, hence the application
of the iteration operator is legit. We next show that
u=\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle

satisfies the following equation

\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444ptu=(\mathsf{id}+\lat u)\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}.

From (
2
), recall that

\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\hskip 3.44444pt\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}).

Also, using the fact that
\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}
and
\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}
are isomorphisms, it is easy to
show that
(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta=\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\Delta+\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta
. Therefore

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}u

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}(D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}),\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}(D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\rangle

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\langle\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}+D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\bigl((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})+(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad(\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{dstl}})\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{dstr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\bigl((\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})+(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}+D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad(\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}+\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}})\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\;=[\mathsf{id}+\hat{\tau},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}[\tau,\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau]]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\bigl(\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}}\Delta\hskip 3.44444pt(\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})+\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\Delta\hskip 3.44444pt(D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}}\times D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}})\bigr)\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\;=[\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\operatorname{\mathsf{inr}}\hskip 3.44444pt\lat\hat{\tau}^{\star}\hskip 3.44444pt\tau\hskip 3.44444pt\langle D\operatorname{\mathsf{fst}},D\operatorname{\mathsf{snd}}\rangle]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}

\displaystyle\;=(\mathsf{id}+\lat u)\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}.

Now

\displaystyle wu

\displaystyle\;=[\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}\operatorname{\mathsf{inl}},\,\lat\operatorname{\mathsf{now}}\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}]\hskip 3.44444pt\operatorname{\mathsf{out}}u
